A hand mutation pass over `island.cpp`, `scenario.cpp`, `save.cpp`, the island part of `state_fields.hpp`, `sim.cpp` and `archive.hpp` (about 90 mutations: every comparison of `validate_islands`, `check_island_spec` and the tiles reader at both sides of its edge, the rectangle overlap tests, the tile and chunk index formulas, the digest field order and the clipping, each setter's dirty mark, every check of `World::create`, every check of the frame, the field order of the drought record) had seven survivors in the first round, all of which got tests: the byte-count division of the tiles reader (a stream short by less than a tile), the island-slot bound of the reader (a million zero bytes behind a hostile count), the drought record's field order (a byte-layout test), the exclusive bottom edge of a deposit, the two vertical-adjacency terms of the rectangle overlap (islands that touch with partly shared x ranges) and the zero rule of the terrain digest (`nonzero_digest` is a function of its own now). The mutants that remain are equivalent (`size() + 0`, a skipped branch that the next check repeats). Not run: GCC, Clang, the sanitizers, the CI jobs.
- **S5 review round (2026-10-19).** Two reviewers' findings (F30 completed, F31): the rectangle checks (inside the world, total area, disjoint) run on the scenario before any tile column is allocated, with a test that the executor is never asked to run for an oversized island; `island_tiles_within_bound` is the one comparison of the per-island bound (exactly the bound accepted, one tile more refused) and a `static_assert` ties the bound to the `Slot` width; hashing a const world never writes the cache (`prepare_hash` and `refresh` are non-const) with a test of four threads hashing one const world; `chunk_of` is total; dropped tile writes are counted (`ignored_writes`, zero for every created world); the loader validates reachable island states only (strict `<` for the drought's phase, step and counter, chunk-aligned sweep positions and drought centre, ring in 0 .. target, city mask within the city slots and a drought refused above 32 city slots, `reserved_object_count` 0); a section failure of a frame names the file offset where the section starts; `World::save()` documents and asserts the terrain precondition; OQ-060 (terrain shape), OQ-061 (island and ring members not modelled), O-54, O-55 and the evidence for O-11 were recorded (`docs/re/islands.md`). Tests for the surviving mutants: the exact tile bound on the stream reader (`tile columns need 67108864 x 13 bytes` and not `more than`) and on the scenario (1 x 2^26 refused as outside the world, not as too many tiles), the byte layout of the terrain blobs with columns of different counts and widths and an island without terrain between two with, a custom PRNG table of the original length (saved as custom), and a table count that fits as bytes but not as `u16` entries. The 243 cases pass with and without game files. Not run: GCC, Clang, the sanitizers, the CI jobs.

**S6. Buildings, cities, creation, invariants.** `slot_list` use in `building.hpp`/`building.cpp`, `city.hpp`, `create.cpp` (`create_building`/`remove_building`, grids, `by_id`, derived counters), `invariants.cpp`, `reject.hpp`, point/rect lookup API, building and city scopes in `state_fields.hpp`; `World::create` places cities and buildings in file order (tick 0, no cost). Tests (`building_state_test.cpp`, `create_remove_test.cpp`, `world_state_test.cpp`): the create/remove cases of §14.2, "rect query canonical order and list masks", "perturbation moves the hash for every field", "field count canary per struct", "save load save with buildings", "check_invariants detects corrupted counters", "allocation pattern independence" (junk-allocation interleaving), **one perturbation test per structural check of §9.6** ("invariant payload alternative vs type", "invariant payload vector sizes vs type counts", "invariant config-sized container sizes", "invariant climate index", "invariant mine without outputs", "invariant island rect"; each also fed through `load`), "island and city query accessors equal the world members", the S2 container probe re-pointed at the real `Building`/`Payload`. Files also: `ClassInfo::payload` use in `invariants.cpp`. Exit: a scenario with buildings can be created, saved, loaded, hashed; `advance` ticks nothing new yet. **Status 2026-10-19: implemented on MSVC and GCC 13 (WSL), not on Clang or the CI jobs (see below).**

**S6 as implemented (2026-10-19).** Files: `reject.hpp`, `building.hpp` / `building.cpp`, `city.hpp`, `create.hpp` / `create.cpp`, `invariants.cpp` (`World::check_invariants` and the three world queries), `island.hpp` / `island.cpp` (`Island::cities`, `Island::cache`, the queries, `validate_cities`), `scenario.hpp` / `scenario.cpp` (`CitySpec`, `BuildingSpec`, cities and buildings in `World::create`), `state_fields.hpp` (the field lists of `StatusTracker`, the six payloads, `Building`, `BuildingList`, `BuildingIndex`, `CityStock`, `GoodsSetting`, `BuildGoods`, `TradeEntry`, `City`, the scope `cities` of the island; the derived rebuild after every state read), `save.cpp` (rebuild and `check_invariants` after `load`), `world.hpp` (stream version 104), `synthetic.hpp` / `synthetic.cpp` (`plain_city`, `g1_scenario`; `basic_scenario` and `two_island_scenario` carry the cities their owner runs name), `tools/sim_stream.py` (the city and building streams, the golden `s6_g1_*`), `tools/lint_sim_allow.txt` (the two byte constants), tests `create_remove_test.cpp`, `building_state_test.cpp`, `world_state_test.cpp`, `building_helpers.hpp`, `world_access.hpp` (`create`, `remove`), `container_probe_test.cpp` re-pointed at the real `Inert`, `Payload`, `Building`, `City` and `Island`. Choices where the spec left room are in §4.6 to §4.8, §7.3, §9.6 and §10 ("as implemented" paragraphs); in short:
- **The stream** gained the scope `cities` in each island (version 104): per city slot a scope `city`, per list a scope `list` inside the scope `index`, per list slot a scope `building`; the derived members (`service_counters`, `house_counts`, `IslandIndexCache`, `StatusTracker::shows_icons`, `WeaponsWorkshop::unlocked`) are not in it. All goldens were re-baselined from `tools/sim_stream.py`; `kStateHashVersion` is pinned to 104 in `config_test.cpp` and `sim_test.cpp`; the S5 scenarios carry one empty city per island because a tile owner must now be a present city (S5 tests that built owner runs without cities call `island_test::add_owner_cities`; tests that re-dimension a tile layer make its tiles neutral first).
- **Issue-rule items.** F32, F33 and F34 are tagged `fix` (own-design hardening; nothing the original accepts is refused). D23 was `decision-needed` (the rect membership rule) and was withdrawn in the review round, the rule being readable; D24 (the footprint tiles' previous object, OQ-063) is `decision-needed` with the stand-in as the default.
- **S6 review round (2026-10-19, two reviewers, 16 fix and 1 decision-needed finding).** Fixed in the stage: (F34) the derived index no longer sizes counters per city SLOT (1.08 GB per island at the bounds) but per present city, and `list_initial_size` is bounded through the product with the list count (test: "the derived index is sized by the cities the state holds", an operator-new probe `alloc_probe.cpp`; "config validate rejects each bad field"); `GoodsSetting` is one signed byte, the trade-list index (0x1002dbd0, 0x1002db53, 0x1002fa11), so `limit` and `SimConfig::trade_limit_max` are gone from the model, the stream and the configuration, and `validate_cities` checks the list indexes and the goods of the trade entries ("invariant trade data"); `rect_query` is allocation-free but for `out`; `house_group` numbers the groups on across several house blocks and the shipped counter index is documented as the BGruppeNr byte; the service counters count the public list only (D: Create 0x10024e18..0x1002500f); `byte0e = 0x82` follows the House class, not the list (D: the house base constructor runs for kinds 97..101 only); the placement stamp writes `status.time` only for the classes that have the slot (Inert keeps 0, DualWorkshop has it); `create_building` re-checks the payload it built and refuses a given mine that names a deposit the island does not have; the rect membership rule was readable (0x10023380, 0x100230d0), D23 is withdrawn and OQ-062 closed. New tests for the rules the first mutation pass left unprotected: a rotated removal (4 rotations, a neighbour on the other orientation's tiles), rotation and `byte0f` of scenario buildings, a given mine's deposit, the service counter 31 and a non-public type with a service mask, the lookup order (an injected overlap), two house blocks, tile objects in the 600-step random run with all rotations. Decision for the owner: D24 (OQ-063) the footprint tiles' previous object. Mutation pass 3 (18 mutations of the new rules, each killed).
- **Queries take the `ListLayout`** (`Island` holds no configuration), see §4.7.
- **Tests** (299 cases in all; S6 added the cases of `create_remove_test.cpp`, `building_state_test.cpp` and `world_state_test.cpp` and changed the existing ones whose expectations moved with the stream version, the island layout or the tile-owner rule): `last == u16(now)` for every class, `status.time` for the classes that have it, and the wrap at 2^16, the payload and the list of every class and its vector sizes, the flag bytes, a given payload, the mine deposit (shallow and deep choose differently, the position is set before the search, the reach table), the rotation of a 3 x 2 footprint, a 2 x 12 footprint, every refusal without a state change and the accepted edge, overlap in any list and across cities, per-island serials, never reused, exhaustion at both ends, removal (hint, cursor, length, id, grid, tile, stale keys), the free-slot policy and its wrap and growth, the derived counters and their inverse, `defeat_pending`, the rect query (order, masks, dedupe, clipping, other cities and islands), `building_at` / `find_building`, the island, city and world accessors against each other, the scenario in file order with the semantics of G1, ascending city numbers, the 17 scenario refusals with their paths; the byte layout of every payload (hand transcription, trace, raw hash, read-back, every prefix, an open tag, status code and bool), 87 single-field perturbations that move only the islands scope and no two to the same hash, the derived members outside hash and save, the hash tree down to the building, save/load/save with holes and a grown list and a continued run, the 12 corrupted derived facts, allocation-pattern independence, a 600-step random create/remove run against the original's insert loop for both policies with `check_invariants` after each step, the golden `s6_g1_*`, and the `load` of every structural violation.
- Verification: MSVC `/W4 /WX` (the whole tree), 294 cases with and without `ANNO1503_GAME_DIR`; GCC 13.3 (WSL) at `-O2` with the CI warning set and `-Werror` (it found one `-Wdangling-reference` in the tests, fixed) and at `-O1` under `-fsanitize=address,undefined,float-cast-overflow -fno-sanitize-recover=all` (clean); `tools/lint_sim.py` clean; `tools/sim_stream.py --check` agrees. A hand mutation pass (one source mutation, GCC rebuild, the whole suite) over `create.cpp`, `building.cpp`, `building.hpp`, `island.cpp`, `scenario.cpp`, `invariants.cpp` and the field lists of `state_fields.hpp`: 105 mutations in the first round, 9 survivors; one was equivalent (`outputs.back()` for a one-output mine) and one was a harness gap (header changes were not rebuilt), the others got tests (a type with id 0, `remove_building` with a key of another island, house groups by block and by class, a mine type with a 1 x 4 footprint, a stale id map, the city number / owner order of the stream); the second round (7 + 14 mutations, headers rebuilt) killed all of them. The `ListFull` return is unreachable in a test (a list of 2^32 - 1 slots) and is not mutation-checked. Not run: Clang, the CI jobs.

**S7. Vertical slice: workshop and farm through `advance`.** `tick.hpp` (`TickContext` with `IslandsView`, `Effects` including `GoodsMove`/`Reserve`/`Deliver`, `SerialWorld`, `SimOutput`), `seams.hpp` with `ctx` on every seam, `scheduler.cpp` (`advance`, `island_tick`, city ring, city tick, building scheduler, object-manager shell, 300 s event with `start_drought`, effect merge), `driver.cpp` (`tick_building` for Workshop and Farm; other ported kinds temporarily behave as `None` via a documented mask removed in S11; `consume`), `figure.hpp` (registry create only), `DrawSource` implementations and telemetry. Tests (`scheduler_test.cpp`, `slice_test.cpp`): subsystem call order with recording seams, island-tick order, zero-chunk island still runs the ring (A1), city-ring counts, building-scheduler order and shared stamp, stalls never visited, holes visited but not ticked, n = 40 000, G1 and its semantic asserts, spawn merge in island order, "effects equal synchronous", 300 s event draw rules (the drought scans use `neutral_owner`, `city_slots`, `roles.market_list`, `chunk_edge`), "recording seams carry state through ctx", "tick_building with a mismatched payload returns without throwing" (corrupt copy through `WorldTestAccess`; release-mode behaviour), "object manager stand-in on empty deposits", "advance zero ms runs subsystems", "step size is not invariant". Exit: weaver and farm produce at the F2/F6 rates through the real scheduler; G1 equal on all platforms. **Status 2026-10-19: implemented on MSVC and GCC 13.3 (WSL), not on Clang or the CI jobs (see below).**

**S7 as implemented (2026-10-19).** Files: `tick.hpp`, `figure.hpp` / `figure.cpp`, `driver.hpp` / `driver.cpp`, `island_tick.hpp`, `scheduler.cpp` (`World::advance`, `Scheduler::merge`, the island phase), `draw.hpp` (`CountingDraw`, `draw_records_between`, `kDrawSiteCount`), `state_fields.hpp` (the field lists of `IdCounters`, `Figure`, the figure slot list, `FigureRegistry`, `TimedEvent`, `EventQueue`; `StateIO` split into eight sections), `save.cpp` (eight sections), `world.hpp` / `sim.cpp` (members `ids_`, `figures_`, `events_`, `out_events_`, `out_`; accessors `ids()`, `figures()`, `events()`, `last_output()`, `drain_events()`; the canary binds 14 members; stream version 105), `invariants.cpp`, `scenario.cpp` (the registry is sized from the configuration), `tools/sim_stream.py` (the new scopes, an independent transcription of the island tick, `IslandSim`), `tools/lint_sim_allow.txt` (two job codes and the draw-site count), tests `scheduler_test.cpp`, `slice_test.cpp`, `slice_helpers.hpp` and `world_access.hpp` (`ids`, `figures`, `events`). Choices where the spec left room:
- **The stream grew by three scopes although the stage list does not name them**: the merge creates figures and queues events, and a field can never be saved and not hashed (§9.1), so `ids` (only the two counters), `figures` and `events` are in the stream from S7 (version 105, all goldens re-baselined from `tools/sim_stream.py`; the `s5_islands_step100` and `s6_g1_step100` goldens also moved because `advance` now ticks the islands, and the script reproduces them with its own transcription of the sweep cursors, the object manager, the city ring and the building scheduler). The dispatch of the event queue, the registry tick and the removal of figures stay S12.
- **`tick_building` has no `ListId`** (nothing needs it; an unused parameter is a warning) and `consume` takes `const Island&` / `const City&` (it reads only the island index, the city number and owner); `has_prod` is not tested again (the registry refuses a ticking class without production data, F25).
- **Merge details** are in §4.11 ("Implemented in S7"); the telemetry is per call: `Effects::draws` is filled by the world from a counting `DrawSource`, summed per site in `SimOutput::draws` (enumerator order) next to the PRNG cursor before and after; `SimOutput` and the host queue are not state (the canary names them and `fields(World)` does not).
- **The island tick** follows §5.4 with the two stage gaps named there (no growth body, no drought machine) and with the message manager not modelled (O-52); the object manager seam is called once per visit before the cursor moves, so a seam reads the object it is on from `objmgr.cursor`; a ring cursor at or beyond the table visits nothing and still writes the stamp (A8 analogue).
- **`check_invariants`** gained the ids, the figure registry (`validate_figures`), the event queue (sequence numbers below `next_event_seq`) and the counter equality of §9.6 (every counter of figures out equals the figures that name the building and the counter; a figure whose home is gone is no error). A scenario whose given payloads carry counters therefore needs figures (the loader of S14 zeroes them).
- **Issue-rule items.** F35 is tagged `fix` (own-design hardening; nothing the original accepts is refused). No `decision-needed` item arose: the stand-ins are O-59 (own design, no visible effect before S12), the growth body and the drought machine are the S13 gaps already in the plan, `Rules::drought_random_centre` is OQ-042 (the option exists since S3 and still selects nothing: the owner must say what a random centre is), and D12 explains why G1's farm and weaver stop after one figure each (the figure never returns, OQ-047).

Mutation pass: about 220 hand mutations of `scheduler.cpp`, `driver.cpp`, `figure.cpp`, the S7 parts of `invariants.cpp`, `state_fields.hpp` and `tick.hpp`; the first rounds had 24 survivors, of which the equivalent or redundant ones were removed or documented (the id-space guard, `has_prod`, the `valid()` clause, the `drain` clear, `delta > 0` versus `>= 0`, the saturation edge `a > max - b`, and two out-of-range reads that only a sanitizer sees) and the others got tests (saturation edges, reserve and delivery edges, 64-bit ticks, telemetry sums and order, owner and city of spawns, Volk of the farm, the scope ordinal of a read error, the ring modulus, `start_drought_at` replacing a record).

Not run: Clang, the CI jobs.
- **S7 review round (2026-10-19, two reviewers, 12 fix and 2 decision-needed findings).** Fixed in the stage (F35 extended, one test per rule): the id counters accept 2^64 - 1 as the used-up state in the loader and `check_invariants` (a spawn at the last valid id used to leave a state both rejected), a refused spawn takes the raised home counter back, `EventQueue::enqueue` saturates the due tick and refuses at sequence exhaustion; `CityStock::add` forms its sum in u64; the host event queue is bounded (`World::set_event_capacity`, `events_dropped`); a slot list read has a memory budget (`kSlotListLoadBytes`); `validate_figures` checks duplicates in `expiry` / `deferred` and the flags against the lists; `FixedPos` is tied to `kWorldEdgeBound` by a `static_assert`; `Rules::validate` rejects `drought_random_centre` as reserved (OQ-042: it used to be accepted and do nothing). New tests for rules the first mutation pass left unprotected: a hole in front of the first market building and a city whose market list is only holes (`continue` mutated to `break` survived), the birth tick above 2^16 (a 16-bit `born` survived), the draw telemetry sort, a huge goods amount on a non-empty stock, a timed event with a delay of 2^64 - 1, the refused-spawn counter, the host queue cap, the load budget; the G1 test checks the conservation of wool and raw material and the step counts at every checkpoint, and the four `s7_g1_*` goldens are labelled self-baselined (they detect change only; `tools/sim_stream.py` does not transcribe the production steps). Decisions for the owner: OQ-064 (the figure kind of a building without a figure id); OQ-042 stays open (the option is now reserved). `tools/disasm.py` documents that `python -I` hides the user site-packages.

**S8. Commands I: codec, apply for SetSpeed and SetProductionLine, privatisation.** `command.hpp`, `codec.cpp`, `apply.cpp`; the last public `World` members (`clock`, `rng`) become private with const accessors (`WorldTestAccess` already exists since S5); `Seams::ai` applies commands; `sim_test.cpp:193` migrated. Tests (`command_codec_test.cpp`, `command_apply_test.cpp`): codec round trips and fuzz, "decode rejects length and count lies", "legacy raw never carries a native tag", "set production line changes output", "rejected command leaves the hash unchanged", "issuer not owner rejected", "unknown ids rejected", "set speed host only", "line not found". Exit: S1 can be driven only through `apply` and `advance`.

**S8 as implemented (2026-10-20).** Files: `command.hpp` (the bodies of §8.3, `Command`, `TurnEntry`, the native record types as the enumeration `WireType`, `kCommandWireVersion` = 1, `kMaxRecordPayload`), `codec.cpp` (`encode_record`, `decode_record`, `encode_turn_entry`, `decode_turn_entry`, `wire_type`), `apply.hpp` / `apply.cpp` (`struct Commands`, the friend of `World`, and `World::apply`), `world.hpp` / `sim.cpp` / `save.cpp` / `scenario.cpp` / `scheduler.cpp` / `state_fields.hpp` (`clock_`, `rng_` private, accessors `clock()` and `rng()`, `friend struct Commands`), `player.hpp` / `player.cpp` (`PlayerHooks::ai`), `scheduler.cpp` (`Scheduler::run_ai`), `tests/world_access.hpp` (`WorldTestAccess::clock`, `::rng`, `::host_events`), tests `command_codec_test.cpp` and `command_apply_test.cpp` (24 new cases, 427 in all), and `tools/lint_sim.py` (`kCommandWireVersion` joins the sentinels next to `kSaveVersion`). **No change of the state stream:** the speed is `settings.speed_percent` (S3), the switches are payload fields of the building scopes (S1..S6); `kStateHashVersion` stays 105 and every golden is unchanged. Choices where the spec left room:
- **The codec is canonical.** `decode_record(encode_record(b)) == b` and, for every input that decodes, `encode_record(decode_record(x)) == x` (the fuzz test asserts both over every value of every byte of every sample and 100 000 random records). Therefore the fourth byte of a placement (the original's unused byte [9]) must be 0 and the `on` byte 0 or 1 (own design, N: a flipped reserved byte must not pass silently); a counted record has exactly `head + n * element` payload bytes, with `n` compared against `remaining / element` before any allocation (a count of 2^32 - 1 allocates nothing); a record has exactly its header plus `len` bytes (`decode_record` takes one record, no trailing bytes). Rotation above 3, unknown type ids and goods ids are NOT decode errors (apply rejects them with the reason, §8.2 item 6); the codec only refuses what cannot be a record.
- **Native types never decode to `LegacyRawCmd`**, and `encode_record` refuses a `LegacyRawCmd` whose type is native (test over all 65 536 types). The original's entry add `0x8a` stays a legacy record; its meaning is `SetTradeSlotCmd::index == kAppendIndex` on `0x73`. The six wire types and the size constants of the codec are enumerations, not `constexpr` variables, because they are format identifiers and layout widths (the lint's `constexpr_count` rule is about game counts); `kCommandWireVersion` is a version and is a lint sentinel. A record holds at most 65 535 payload bytes (its length is a `u16`): `encode_record` returns an error for a longer body (a `Build` of more than 4 095 placements must be split by its sender; 12 + 16 · 4 095 = 65 532 bytes; the first version of this note said 4 093, corrected in S10).
- **`World::apply` checks in this order:** the issuer holds a player slot (`UnknownIssuer`, for every body including `LegacyRaw`); then per body. `SetSpeed`: `NotHost` (`issuer == cfg.host_player`), `BadSpeed` (`is_original_speed(percent, cfg.multiplayer)`), then the store. `SetProductionLine`: `UnknownIsland` (no island rectangle holds the tile), `UnknownCity` (the tile's owner is `neutral_owner` or an empty city slot), `NotCityOwner` (`city.owner != issuer`), `NoBuildingAtTile` (`Island::building_at` over `lookup_order` for that city, so a building whose footprint covers the tile counts, not only its north-west tile), `UnknownType` (cannot happen in a state that passes `check_invariants`), `LineNotFound` (the goods is none of the type's `output_goods`, the class has no switch, or the index is beyond the lines of the payload). The switch is the one of §8.4: `Workshop`, `Farm`, `Mine` one `switched_on`, `WeaponsWorkshop` and `SmallFarm` `line_on[i]` for output `i`; a `Workshop` with several outputs switches as a whole. Setting a switch to the value it has is accepted and changes nothing. `Build`, `Demolish`, `SetTradeFlag`, `SetTradeSlot` and `LegacyRaw` answer `Unsupported` (S10 gives the first four their rules; the fifth stays closed), with no `records`.
- **A rejection is told to the host, not to the state:** `apply` queues a `SimEventKind::CommandRejected` event (`player` = the issuer, `code` = the `Reject` value, `tick` = the clock's tick) in the host queue, and a rejection inside `advance` (an AI command) is also in `SimOutput::events`. An accepted command queues nothing. The hash does not see the queue (test: hash and save bytes before == after for 3 000 random commands).
- **The AI seam** is `PlayerHooks::ai`, a plain function pointer called by `player_table_tick` once, after the visits and before the stamp is written (D 0x1009c4d0), whatever the table holds. `Scheduler::run_ai` builds a `SerialWorld` over a fresh `Effects`, calls `Seams::ai` (which draws through `TickContext::draw`, `DrawSite::Ai`, counted in `SimOutput::draws`), merges the effects (C2), then applies each returned command in order through `Commands::apply`, the code `World::apply` uses (A15); nobody logs them. The `issuer` of an AI command is whatever the seam sets: a command that names a player who does not own the city is rejected like a host command.
- **Cost:** `apply` is O(size of the command) plus the island count (the island is found by scanning the island table, as `World::island_at` does; the derived grid stays a later optimisation) and one `std::map` lookup in the island's id cache. It scans no building and no player list, and an accepted command allocates nothing (test with the operator-new probe).
- **Tests:** codec: the pinned variant order and native types, the exact bytes of every native record, the identity over 16 samples, `TurnEntry`, every wrong length of every native record, hostile counts (nine values, with an allocation bound), flag and reserved bytes, all 65 536 types for the legacy rule, the `u16` length limit at the exact edge, prefixes, suffixes, all 256 values of every byte, 100 000 random records. Apply: the closed world (`static_assert`s that `clock()` and `rng()` have no writer), speed (host, original speeds only, the multiplayer table, the host as configuration), unknown issuers, "set production line changes output" (a switched-off weaver makes nothing in 10 s of game time while its twin makes steps, and makes steps again after the switch goes on), the switch of every class, line not found, unknown ids and tiles, issuer not owner (both directions and after an owner change), the later-stage commands, the rejected-hash property over 3 000 random commands with every reason hit, the host events, zero allocation, and the AI seam (commands applied inside `advance` in order, rejections reported, the stamp the seam sees, one draw per call, a quiet seam changes nothing).
- Verification: MSVC `/W4 /WX` (the whole tree), 427 sim cases with and without `ANNO1503_GAME_DIR`, the full `ctest -j 16` (617 tests; two render game-file tests that do not link the sim failed once in the loaded full run and passed alone); GCC 13.3 (WSL) at `-O3` with the CI warning set and `-Werror` over `formats`, `test_support`, `sim` and the sim tests, 427 cases with and without the game files; clang-format 23.1.3 clean over `src`; `tools/lint_sim.py` ok and its self-test. ASan + UBSan: g++ 13 in WSL with `-fsanitize=address,undefined -fno-sanitize-recover=all`, 427 of 427 with and without the game files (T7 and T7b; the `asan` preset itself cannot be configured in WSL). CI (PR #14, merged 2026-10-22): every job green, Clang `-Werror` and Clang ASan/UBSan (`linux-clang-asan`) included, except `macos-clang`, which failed on the known timing-dependent GPU river-tier test only (T13).
- **Review (2026-10-20).** A hand mutation pass over `apply.cpp`, `scheduler.cpp`, `player.cpp` and `codec.cpp` (28 mutations, one at a time, GCC 13 at `-O1`, the whole suite): the issuer, host, speed and owner checks, the city number of the lookup, the switch index of the weapons workshop and of the small farm, the sense of the farm switch, the stored speed, the host event, its code and tick, the copy of the event in the advance output, the AI hook (off, after the stamp, commands not applied, effects not merged), and in the codec the exact count, the reserved byte, the flag byte, trailing bytes, a length beyond the input, the payload limit, the native-type refusal of `encode_record`, the `on`, `mode` and `byte7` fields and the short turn entry. All 28 were killed. One equivalent mutation was left out of the list (removing the `neutral_owner` test: an empty city slot gives `UnknownCity` as well). No `decision-needed` item arose (everything above is own design, N, or follows §8). The task board asks for two independent reviewers; the session that implemented S8 could not start a second agent, so no one but the author has read the diff: the orchestrator should run them (the mutation list above is a starting point).

**S9. Replay and session.** `replay.hpp`/`replay.cpp` (`ReplayLog`, `Recorder`, `run_turn`, `run_frame`, divergence finder), `PrintArchive`, `src/tools/sim_replay/` (`run`, `hashes`, `first-diff`, `tree`, `golden`) with its CMake entry. Tests (`replay_test.cpp`): "recorder replay reproduces checkpoints", "write_log read_log round trip", "first divergent entry", "diff_trees names the corrupted building", "replay refuses a config mismatch", "run_turn applies commands before steps", "log header records the prng timeline". Exit: S1 driven only through `Recorder`; replay reproduces every checkpoint. **Carried over from the T7b review (requirements for this stage):** (1) add the `record_size(span)` / `decode_turn(span, std::vector<TurnEntry>&)` framing helper (§8.5, §8.7) so the version read and the per-turn record framing live in one place — the replay-log header carries `kCommandWireVersion` once, not per record; (2) an AI-issued `SetSpeed` from inside `advance` stays allowed (spec §8.2 item 4): the `Seams::ai` seam is trusted code, not a rules question, so no extra check is added. A further requirement belongs to M4, not S9: the net layer drops a peer after N consecutive decode errors (`decode_record` is total and canonical on hostile input, so a decode error is the peer's fault, not the stream's) — noted here so the M4 handshake work does not forget it.

**S9 as implemented (2026-10-22).** Files: `replay.hpp` / `replay.cpp` (`ReplayEntry` with its `Kind` {Advance, Commands, Checkpoint}, `ReplayLog`, `Recorder`, `Turn`, `run_turn`, `run_frame`, `write_log` / `read_log`, `ReplayOutcome` and `replay`, `hash_per_entry`, `first_divergent_entry`, `tree_at`, `diff_trees`, `PrintArchive`, the helpers `seams_digest` and `prng_timeline_missing`, and the frame-version constant `kReplayLogVersion` = 1), the framing helper `record_size(span)` / `decode_turn(span, std::vector<TurnEntry>&)` in `command.hpp` / `codec.cpp`, the draw-site manifest `draw_site_name` / `draw_site_ported` in `draw.hpp`, `src/tools/sim_replay/` (`anno-sim-replay`: `run`, `hashes`, `first-diff`, `tree` with `--entry`/`--depth`/`--dump-scope`, `golden`) with its CMake entry and `add_subdirectory(sim_replay)`, `tests/replay_test.cpp` (the seven named cases plus five: the frame pinned byte for byte against the layout below, the framing helper over a back-to-back stream with every prefix, a hostile/truncated `read_log` over every prefix and garbage, `PrintArchive` full, filtered and with a `diff_trees` path, and `hash_per_entry`/`tree_at` following the log), and `tools/lint_sim.py` (`kReplayLogVersion` joins the version sentinels next to `kCommandWireVersion`). **No change of the state stream:** the replay log is a session artifact, nothing in it is hashed or saved; `kStateHashVersion` stays 105 and every golden is unchanged. Choices where the spec left room:
- **The log frame is explicit little-endian through the project archives** (`WriteArchive` / `ReadArchive`), not a hand-rolled cursor. Normative layout (`kReplayLogVersion` = 1; the test "write_log matches the documented frame byte for byte" is transcribed from this item): the four bytes `A3RP`; `u32 kReplayLogVersion`; `u32 kCommandWireVersion` (**once**, not per record, §8.5); `u64 config_digest, u64 rules_digest, u64 registry_digest, u64 seams_digest`; `u32 prng_timeline_version`; `u64 n`, then `n` strings (`u64 length`, then the bytes) for `prng_timeline_missing`; `u64 length`, then the bytes of `initial_save`; `u64 count`, then the entries, each a `u8 kind` (0 Advance, 1 Commands, 2 Checkpoint) followed by: Advance `u32 ms, u64 tick`; Commands `u64 length`, then that many bytes of back-to-back `TurnEntry` records (`u32 issuer, u32 seq, u16 type, u16 len, payload`, §8.5; `seq` is the command's ordinal in the entry), framed on read by `decode_turn` through `record_size`; Checkpoint `u64 tick, u64 hash`. Nothing follows the last entry. `read_log` latches the first error and rejects a bad magic, a wrong version, a length beyond the input, an unknown entry kind, a command block that does not decode, and trailing bytes; every prefix of a valid log and arbitrary garbage are a clean `Result` error, never UB (tested).
- **`ReplayLog::kVersion` is the namespace constant `kReplayLogVersion`**, not a `static constexpr` struct member: a member would read as a game count to `lint_sim` (the regex does not see scope), so the version lives beside `kCommandWireVersion` as a named sentinel, exactly as `kSaveVersion` does.
- **`replay(World&, log)` drives the world it is given** (which the caller builds with the matching registry and seams, at the log's initial state) and does **not** reload `initial_save` — the registry is not in the log, so the world must already carry it. It refuses before any entry runs when the config, rules, registry or seams digest, the PRNG-timeline version or its list of missing draw sites differs from the log's (§8.2 item 4; the list is compared too, so a site ported without a version bump is refused, not replayed with silently different draws), and stops at the first checkpoint whose hash differs. `initial_save` is carried so the **tool** can rebuild the world: `anno-sim-replay` rebuilds it on `TypeRegistry::empty()` (the original goods table, no building types), which fits the headless golden and these tests; a log recorded against another registry reports a digest mismatch there until the scenario path arrives with the game files (S14).
- **The PRNG timeline** is `{kPrngTimelineVersion, missing[]}` where `missing` is the names of the `DrawSite`s the sim does not reproduce yet, in enum order: `CityRandomEvents`, `WildlifeSweep`, `Ai` (the CompLogic AI; the installed AI seam is a stand-in, not the original's draws, §6). `draw_site_name` / `draw_site_ported` in `draw.hpp` are the single manifest this is derived from.
- **T7b carry-overs honoured:** the `record_size` / `decode_turn` framing helper puts the version read and the per-record framing in one place (the helper walks a `TurnEntry` stream and the replay reader reuses it). An AI-issued `SetSpeed` from inside `advance` stays allowed — no extra check was added (`Seams::ai` is trusted code). The net's "drop a peer after N decode errors" item was left to M4.
- **`PrintArchive`** is a fourth write-side archive over the one field list (`fields(a, w)`): one primitive per line, indented by scope depth, with an optional path filter so `--dump-scope world/islands/island[0]/…/building[3]` prints that scope and its descendants (ancestors shown as headers). `diff_trees` returns the real scope path including the `index` scope (e.g. `world/islands/island[0]/cities/city[0]/index/list[4]/building[0]`); the spec's example omitted `index` for brevity. `world` is the hash tree's name for the root, not a scope the field list opens, so the filter accepts the `diff_trees` path verbatim and strips a leading `world` (`world` alone prints the whole stream); the step "`tree` → path → `--dump-scope` path" of the workflow therefore needs no editing of the path (tested).
- Verification: MSVC `/W4 /WX` warning-free (the whole tree); the full `ctest --preset msvc -j 16` 738/738 green with `ANNO1503_GAME_DIR` and 738/738 without; GCC 13.3 (WSL) at `-O3` with the CI warning set and `-Werror` over `formats`, `test_support`, `sim`, the sim tests and the tool's `main.cpp`, no diagnostic, 439 of 439 sim cases with and without the game files; clang-format 23.1.3 clean over `src`; `tools/lint_sim.py` ok and its self-test; `anno-sim-replay` exercised end to end on a recorded log (`run` reproduced 3/3 checkpoints; `hashes`, `golden`, `tree`, and `--dump-scope` with a path copied from `tree` as expected). CI on PR #19 (head `a234232`, before the review fixes below): every job green, `linux-clang-asan` (Clang ASan + UBSan), `macos-clang`, `linux-gcc-release` and `windows-msvc` included.
- **Review (2026-10-22).** Two reviewers read the diff against this section; all acceptance items passed. Reviewer 2's mutation pass found two tests that a mutant could pass, both fixed: the round trip cannot see a layout change that the writer and the reader make together, so the frame is now pinned byte for byte against the layout above (mutant: `config_digest` and `rules_digest` swapped in both — the round trip passes, the pinned test fails); the truncation loop of the framing test asserted nothing (`ok || error non-empty`), so it now requires every prefix that ends on an entry boundary to decode to exactly the entries before it and every other prefix to fail while keeping those entries (mutant: `decode_turn` stops quietly at a cut entry — 86 assertions fail). Also fixed on re-reading: `--dump-scope` did not accept the `world/…` path that `diff_trees` returns (the field list never opens a `world` scope), so the documented workflow printed nothing; `PrintArchive` now strips the root name, and `tree` prints one full path per scope so a line diff of two runs names the path to paste.
- **Orchestrator review fix-up (2026-10-22 h, TASKS.md §4 items 1, 2, 4, 6, 7 and the nits).** (1) `Recorder::submit` refuses a command that has no wire form (`encode_record` refuses it: a `LegacyRawCmd` with a native type, a payload beyond `kMaxRecordPayload`) **before** logging or applying it, with `Reject::Unencodable` (appended to `Reject`, §8.3); `write_log` returns a `Result` and refuses a hand-built log holding such a command (naming entry and command) instead of writing it without the command. Before, the command was applied and then silently dropped by `write_log`, so the written log was no longer the session. Note: `run_turn`/`run_frame` without a recorder still call `World::apply` directly, which answers such a command by its body (today `Unsupported`, a no-op), so the recorder and the direct path agree as long as no unencodable body is applied by the rules (S10's `Build` with more placements than a record can hold would be the first). **Closed in S10:** without a recorder the session drivers skip a command that `has_wire_form` refuses, exactly as `Recorder::submit` does (test "run_turn without a recorder skips a command without a wire form"). (2) `replay` compares `prng_timeline_missing` with this build's list, not only the version. (4) A divergence test over two recorded runs that split at entry 4 (`first_divergent_entry == 4` in both orders; `replay` of the mixed log stops at the next checkpoint). (6) `anno-sim-replay` parses `--entry` and `--depth` with `std::from_chars` over the whole argument; a bad value is a usage error with exit status 2 (CTest `anno_sim_replay_args_*` through `cmake/RunToolExpectError.cmake`). (7) `World::save`'s precondition is stated on `Recorder` and checked by `Recorder::start` (the constructor is private): a world with a digest-only terrain placeholder cannot be recorded. Nits: the comments say that `submit` logs then applies while `advance` advances then logs the tick it reached; `run_turn` with a recorder emits one Commands entry per command (§8.7). Every new test was mutation-checked (the fix reverted, the test fails).

**S10. Build, Demolish, trade commands.** `apply.cpp` (Build, Demolish, SetTradeFlag, SetTradeSlot), `placement.cpp` (stand-in rule), create with charge, `legacy_codec.cpp`, `fixtures/synthetic/sim/g2.log`. Tests (`command_build_test.cpp`, `property_test.cpp`): "build charges gold and goods", "build drag spends in stream order", "build atomic rule", "build footprint owner rules", "build occupied", "build unknown type and rotation", "build flags are not on the wire", "free build cheat rule", "demolish frees the slot and keeps the hint", "demolish skips unowned tiles", "city removal default never", "trade flag modes 0 1 9", "trade slot index", "trade limit and list bounds", "random command streams keep invariants", "G2 replay equals golden", "legacy 0x64 decode on synthetic bytes". Exit: G2 log committed; free-slot reuse after demolish matches the hand-derived order.

**S10 free-slot reuse order, derived by hand before the code (2026-10-23).** The rule is §5.2 (D `0x10026a00`, `0x10026b30`, `0x100255c0`, `Rules::slot_policy = Original`): the scan starts at `(hint + 1) mod n` and takes the first null slot; with none the list grows to `max(n + n/4, list_min_growth)` and the new element takes slot `n`; a removal empties the slot and leaves `hint`, `cursor` and `n` alone. Start: the city of `synthetic::g1_scenario` (S6 G1), whose production list after the load is `[weaver, farm, null × 8]`, `n = 10`, `hint = 1` (§14.3 semantic assert). Every Build below places one 1 × 1 weaver on a free city tile (one record, one command); every Demolish names one tile of the building it removes.

| Step | Command | Scan from | Slot taken | `hint` after | `n` after |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Build B1 | 2 | 2 | 2 | 10 |
| 2 | Build B2 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 10 |
| 3 | Demolish the farm (slot 1) | – | – (slot 1 empties) | 3 | 10 |
| 4 | Build B3 | 4 | **4** (not the freed 1) | 4 | 10 |
| 5 | Build B4, B5, B6, B7, B8 | 5 … 9 | 5, 6, 7, 8, 9 | 9 | 10 |
| 6 | Build B9 | 0 (wraps) | **1** (slot 0 holds the weaver) | 1 | 10 |
| 7 | Build B10 | 2 | none free → growth to 12, slot **10** | 10 | 12 |
| 8 | Demolish B2 (slot 3), then B1 (slot 2) | – | – | 10 | 12 |
| 9 | Build B11 | 11 | 11 (a null of the growth) | 11 | 12 |
| 10 | Build B12 | 0 (wraps) | **2** | 2 | 12 |
| 11 | Build B13 | 3 | 3 | 3 | 12 |

So B1 … B13 take the slots 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 1, 10, 11, 2, 3, the list ends with `n = 12` and `hint = 3`, and the serials are 3 … 15 (the island's counter is never reused). With `SlotPolicy::LowestFree` the same commands give 2, 3, 1, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 2, 3, 11, so a policy mistake shows at step 4. The test "demolish frees the slot and keeps the hint" checks this table row by row. G2 (§14.3) uses the first rows of the same rule: the second weaver at tick 5 000 takes slot 2, a third weaver slot 3, the farm is demolished (slot 1 empties, `hint` stays 3), and the next weaver takes slot 4.

**S10 as implemented (2026-10-23).** Files: `apply.cpp` (Build, Demolish, SetTradeFlag, SetTradeSlot; `Commands::apply` takes an `InAdvance*` instead of a flag), `placement.hpp` / `placement.cpp` (`footprint_owner_rule`, `stand_in_placement_rule`), `legacy_codec.hpp` / `legacy_codec.cpp` (`legacy::decode_0x64`), `scheduler_internal.hpp` (the declaration of `Scheduler`, moved out of `scheduler.cpp` so the commands can merge Effects; `merge` gained `in_advance`), `codec.cpp` / `command.hpp` (`has_wire_form`), `replay.cpp` (the session drivers' rule), `synthetic.hpp` / `synthetic.cpp` (`make_build_types`, `g2_scenario`), `src/tools/sim_replay/main.cpp` (the registry by digest) with the CTest `anno_sim_replay_g2_run`; tests `command_build_test.cpp` (15 of the 17 named cases and five more from the review: "build charges a goods named twice in full", "build enforces research only under the rule", "build and demolish run the place and remove seams", "build placement rule and upgrade seams", "demolish leaves another city's building on the city's tile"), `property_test.cpp` ("random command streams keep invariants", "G2 replay equals golden", and "run_turn without a recorder skips a command without a wire form"), `command_helpers.hpp`; fixtures `fixtures/synthetic/sim/g2.log` (binary, `.gitattributes`) and the golden `s10_g2_end`. **No change of the state stream:** every field the commands write existed (money, stock, `build_goods[].spent` and `.setting`, the goods settings, the trade lists, the lists and the tiles); `kStateHashVersion` stays 105 and every earlier golden is unchanged. Choices where §8.4 left room (all own design, N, unless a line cites D or I):
- **Build, the order of the checks per record** is §8.4's: type id 0 skipped (its record says `None` with no building); `UnknownType`, `BadRotation` (F4); a type without a list `Unsupported` (O-4); `footprint_owner_rule` (`OutOfIsland`, then `TileNotOwned`: a tile of another city, two owners, or no city tile at all; D `0x10058890`); the placement rule (the seam, else the D17 stand-in: a tile with a non-zero object is `Occupied`, which also covers every building, whose tiles carry its type id, O-5); `create_building`'s own overlap check (F32: a building of any city under the footprint is `Occupied`), repeated here so that a placement seam that ignores buildings cannot make a validated record fail at its creation (review fix); `Seams::upgrade_rewrite` (a rewritten record is validated again from the type check, once); `Rules::enforce_research` (`NotResearched`, off); the serial (`SerialExhausted`); affordability (`CannotAfford`, D `0x100275d0`; the goods of a type are summed per goods number first, so a type that names one goods twice is charged in full, review fix). Then `create_building` at the clock's tick (`now` = the tick's low 32 bits, F1) with `byte0f` = 0, the charge and `Seams::on_placed`. **The charge follows the creation** in code: nothing reads money or stock in between, so the state is the original's charge-then-Create (D `0x100279a0`), and a creation that fails after validation (only `ListFull`, a list of 2³² slots) leaves nothing to refund (F18).
- **The cost waiver** is `owner.free_build` (D owner slot `+0x9c(7)`, O-9) or `Rules::free_build_cheat` (F13: the original's free-build flag `0x2000000` came from the payload; here it is a hashed session rule for every player). A waived build skips the affordability check **and** the charge and adds nothing to `build_goods[].spent` (I: "cost waiver"; whether the original's `+0x9c(7)` skips only one of them is OQ-126). Who the cheat rule should cover is the owner's (OQ-125); the default is off.
- **What a Build reports.** One `RecordResult` per placement. The command is refused (status = the first failing record's reason) only when no record was placed and at least one failed, so "rejected ⇒ unchanged" holds; a partial drag build (D8) and a Build of type-0 records only (or none) are accepted. A refused Build queues one `CommandRejected` event like every refused command; a partial one queues none (its records say which failed).
- **`Rules::build_atomic`** (D8 opt-in): a dry run validates every record against a scratch budget (`Budget`: the gold and goods of the earlier records, their serials, and the island tiles their footprints cover, so a record that overlaps an earlier one of the same command is `Occupied`). One failure places nothing: the failing records keep their reason, the others say `AtomicAbort`, the command status is `AtomicAbort`. When all pass, the live loop repeats the checks record by record (only a Place seam's Effects could make them differ).
- **The Place and Remove seams** (O-4, O-5) run with the advance's `TickContext` and counting draw source when the command is applied inside an advance (the AI seam's commands, `InAdvance`), and between two advances with a context at the clock's tick over the world PRNG (`GlobalDraw` through a `CountingDraw`); their Effects merge at once (C2) through `Scheduler::merge`, which between advances leaves `SimOutput` alone (it belongs to the last advance) and queues the UI events for the host only. Defaults: none.
- **Demolish** never refuses after the city checks (`UnknownIsland`, `UnknownCity`, `NotCityOwner`): each tile gets a record. A tile outside the island (`OutOfIsland`) or not the city's (`TileNotOwned`) is skipped silently (D, op 6 of `0x10058e90`); a building of the city found by `Island::building_at` over the lookup order (D `0x10023c80`) is removed (`Seams::on_removed`, then `remove_building`; the record carries its id); a tile object with no building of any city on the tile is cleared (stand-in O-5, D24, OQ-063); anything else is `NoBuildingAtTile` (also a building of another city standing on this city's tile, which only a scenario can produce: its object is not cleared). Nothing is refunded (manual, `docs/research/playbook.md` §14).
- **City removal** (D9, O-6, O-48): after each tile, while the city exists, `!rules.keep_empty_city && seams.city_is_empty && city_is_empty(city)` removes it: every building through `remove_building` (derived indexes, footprint objects, the market list's `defeat_pending`; no Remove seam), every tile of the city becomes `neutral_owner` (a stand-in so §9.6 "every owner is a present city or neutral" holds; what the original does with the tiles is OQ-126) and the slot is emptied. Later tiles of the same command answer `UnknownCity`. Default: no seam, so no city is ever removed.
- **SetTradeFlag** (D `0x1002dbd0`): `goods < warehouse_goods` else `BadIndex`; modes 0 and 1 store `-1` for `0xffffffff` or, after checking that list `mode` exists and its entry `value` holds the goods (else `BadValue`; the original's byte 0xff is an index here, not -1), the index, and `buy = (mode == 1)`; mode 9 stores `value` as the setting of a build goods and ignores other goods; any other mode is accepted and ignored (the original handles 0, 1 and 9 only).
- **SetTradeSlot** (I, O-14, OQ-127): `list < trade_lists` and `index < size` or `kAppendIndex`, else `BadIndex`; an entry's goods must be below `warehouse_goods` (`BadValue`, §9.6); goods 0 clears an entry in place (all three fields 0, the list keeps its length) and appends nothing (`BadValue`); when an entry changes goods, the goods setting that named it for the old goods is unlinked (`list_index = -1`, as the original's entry removal writes 0xff, `0x100285f4`, `0x10028655`), so every setting keeps naming an entry that holds its goods. The capacity of a list stays open (O-10).
- **The legacy importer** decodes 0x64 only (§8.5): a follower's rotation byte is kept whole (Build refuses a value above 3), the head's is its low two bits; the flags word is read and dropped (F13); type ids have 12 bits. The 0x68 run-length tile list is not imported because §8.5 does not give its record head (OQ-126).
- **The S9 gap is closed** (§15 S9 fix-up note): `has_wire_form(Body)` decides without allocating what `encode_record` accepts (a Build of up to 4 095 placements, a Demolish of up to 8 190 tiles); `Recorder::submit` uses it, and `run_turn` / `run_frame` without a recorder skip such a command instead of applying it.
- **G2** (§14.3): `synthetic::g2_scenario` is the G1 scenario on `make_build_types` (the synthetic types with the build costs of the Anlagen.dat entries they stand for, `docs/research/playbook.md` §14, and test footprints) with 13 t of tools and 30 t of wood. The session (`property_test.cpp`, `record_g2`): 400 single-player advances of 100 ms with six checkpoints; at tick 5 000 a second weaver and three rejected Builds (unknown issuer, a tile object, a neutral tile); its line off and on; a third weaver, the farm demolished, a fourth weaver, a skipped Demolish tile, a trade entry, its goods setting, a build-goods setting, a legacy record; a drag build of four records (placed, `CannotAfford`, type 0, `UnknownType`). Asserts independent of the hash: the slots 2, 3, 4, 5 with slot 1 freed and `hint` 5 (the table above), 4 × 300 gold, 12 t of tools and 16 t of wood spent. The recorded log must equal `g2.log` byte for byte (`ANNO_UPDATE_GOLDEN=1` rewrites it), the committed log replays 6/6 checkpoints in the test and through `anno-sim-replay run`, and `s10_g2_end` is the final hash (self-baselined). The log carries the stream version in its initial save, so a stream bump (S11 on) re-records it in the same commit as the other goldens. `anno-sim-replay` now rebuilds a log's world on the code-built registry whose digest the log names (empty, synthetic, or synthetic with build costs).
- **Review (2026-10-23).** Two reviewers: one read the diff against §8 and this section, one mutation-checked the tests in a private GCC copy (72 mutations). Fixed: (1) the atomic dry run did not repeat `create_building`'s overlap check, so a placement seam that ignores buildings could end an atomic build half done; the check is now part of the validation of every record; (2) a type that names one goods twice was checked per entry and undercharged; the goods are summed per goods number; (3) survivors got tests: the atomic gold and serial budgets, a record only `create_building` would refuse inside the dry run, the Place / Remove / placement-rule / upgrade seams and `merge` between and inside an advance, `Rules::enforce_research`, scenery under a footprint tile other than the north-west one, a duplicate goods on one trade list, `NotCityOwner` for the trade commands, legacy groups of 15 followers and type ids above 2047, a building of another city on the city's tile; (4) `encode_record` asks `has_wire_form`; (5) the manual's two placement rules (a dragged demolish frame clears only plants and roads, nothing is built or demolished with enemy units near) and the figures of removed buildings went into OQ-126, the trade-list capacity into OQ-127; §8.4 names the `BadValue` cases of SetTradeSlot. Mutation evidence on the final code (MSVC, the whole sim suite per mutant): money not charged (7 cases fail, among them "build charges gold and goods", "build drag spends in stream order", "build atomic rule", G2), stock not removed (5), `spent` not added (3), the atomic dry run skipped (3), the atomic gold budget ignored and the serial budget ignored ("build atomic rule"), the slot scan from 0 (11, among them "demolish frees the slot and keeps the hint" and G2), a removal that resets the hint (9); the reviewer's table kills the charge, the atomic rule (dry run, scratch overlap, goods budget, AtomicAbort marking) and the free-slot order on the first version too. Equivalent or unreachable survivors left: a rotation bound of 4 in `check_record` (`create_building` refuses it as well).
- **Verification.** MSVC `/W4 /WX` warning-free over the whole tree; `ctest --preset msvc -j 16` without the game files 774/774; with the game files the full run was stopped by the session's memory guard after 427 of 774 tests had passed (two Python tool tests were cut off with it and pass alone: `anno_dump_agrees_with_python`, `river_qa_gap_survey`), and the sim suite with the game files passes 466/466 on its own; GCC 13.3 `-O3 -Werror` with the CI warning set in WSL over `formats`, `test_support`, `sim`, the sim tests and `anno-sim-replay` (57 TUs) clean, 466/466 with and without the game files, and `g2.log` is byte-identical when recorded by the GCC build; `anno-sim-replay run g2.log` 6/6; clang-format 23.1.3 clean over `src`; `tools/lint_sim.py` ok and its self-test.

Warehouse goods are ids 0..0x37 (city record 12 bytes at `+0x334+12g`; `add` refuses g > 0x37): 55 usable slots of which 47 are named; crops 0x38–0x4b and animals 0x89–0x8d are not warehouse goods. ## Appendix C. PRODTYPE → class, tick, list (D: factory 0x10024010, byte table 0x1002528c (161 entries), jump table 0x1002521c; second switch table 0x10025348/0x10025330)

Every PRODTYPE string of the 921 entries resolves (5 entries are UNUSED: 101, 102, 103, 1003, 1007). Tick gate periods (**D**: the compare constant is read in every class tick; what the body does with `last` after the gate is not read except for MARKTSTAND and the ported production classes; `ClassInfo::gate_period`, hashed in the registry digest): 500 in workshop 0x100680b0, farm/fisher 0x1006ea90, trapper 0x1006e5d0, mine 0x1006def0, 0x1006f660, class 12 0x10068a70, class 17 0x10069550, market classes 0x1006b6d0/0x1006c2e0, 0x100676b0, 0x10067b50, 0x1006cc40, **PublicService and School 0x1006b4e0 (`cmp dx, 0x1f4` at 0x1006b4ec, shared tick; 13 instructions, the MARKTSTAND rule: add the period to `last` once the u16 difference reaches it), Hunter 0x10069f40 (0x10069f5d), Whaler 0x10073720 (0x10073737), Shipyard 0x10074e90 (0x10074ea1)**; 2500 houses 0x10076230; 5000 0x100702d0 and 0x10075608; 166 towers 0x10070c50 (0x10070c62) and **cannon towers 0x1006ab50 (0x1006ab6a, `0xa6`)**. Castle 0x1006f1d0 has no stamp gate (a byte counter at +0x38) and the Quarry class is the stub 0x10001030: both are 0. (Before the S3 review round the periods of PublicService, School, Hunter, Whaler, Shipyard and CannonTower were 0, "unread"; the registry digests and the goldens were re-baselined, `goldens.txt`.)
